Surah: 86. Follow the brief below (surah_map.md) exactly. The evidence is text.md (the surah), dictionary.md (every root of the surah's words, each branch with the classical dictionaries' own phrases), channels.md and hft.md (both are earlier readers' proposals: ignore their judgements (grades, strength or confidence labels, reading types, statements of what a reading may or may not do) and make your own) and your own knowledge of Arabic and the Quran. Return only the map as your final message.

===== _commentary/v16/prompts/r2/surah_map.md =====
Read the surah as one text and write its map of image chains: the lexical images that run through several of
its ayat and join them into one scene, process or movement. A later writer will read one ayah at a time, with
only that ayah's own dictionary. The map lets that writer hear what the ayah's words carry in the surah as a
whole, including senses whose evidence sits under the words of other ayat.

Your evidence is the surah text, the dictionary of every root in the surah (each branch with the classical
dictionaries' own phrases), earlier readers' channel review (channels.md) and activation hypotheses (hft.md),
and your own knowledge of Arabic and the Quran. Where a member or passage comes from memory rather than from
the dictionary or the text, say so. Do not use tools, delegate, browse or inspect files.

The channel review and the HFT records are proposals by earlier readers. Ignore their judgements: grades,
strength or confidence labels, reading types, words such as "surprising", "exploratory" or "latent", and every
statement of what a reading may or may not do. Make your own judgement from the surah's words, the dictionary
phrases and the Quran. Do not rediscover what they already assembled: start from their chains, test each one
against the dictionary phrases and the text, and join, extend, split or correct them. Where their wording
abstracts a member, go back to the dictionary phrase and name what it actually says.

A chain belongs on the map when its members are senses the dictionary attests for words that stand in the
surah, and together they make one image or process that the surah's wording or sequence lets a listener hear.
A member need not be the sense that translates its word in its own ayah; a chain is heard across the surah,
not in one word. Say plainly when a member is a rare sense or is attested only inside a fixed expression, and
keep it. When the dictionary itself joins two of the surah's words in one phrase, quote that phrase: it is
the strongest evidence a chain can have. Keep a scene at the level of its objects, their parts and their
operation. When proposals share members, do not fold one into another's more abstract function unless nothing
concrete is lost. Carry every chain that meets this test, however unusual; leave out proposals that do not.

Write the map in English, with Arabic quoted exactly (surah wording from the text, dictionary phrases from the
dictionary). It is working material for the writer, not commentary prose.

1. `## Chains`. For each chain, a `###` heading naming the image. One paragraph on what the image is and how it
   moves through the surah. Then its members, one line each: the ayah ref, the word as it stands in the text,
   the root and branch id, the dictionary's own phrase quoted exactly, and what this member contributes to the
   image. Add Quran passages outside the surah, with exact refs, where they stage or confirm the chain.
2. `## Ayat`. For each ayah in order: which chains it opens, advances or completes, and what its words add to
   each.
3. `## Not carried`. Each channel subchannel and each HFT record you did not carry into a chain, one line each:
   its name and why.

No ranking and no labels of strength or confidence. No list of what a writer must include. There is no length
target and no required number of chains or members.


===== _commentary/v16/work/s086/surah.r2/text.md =====
# Surah 86

- 86:1 وَٱلسَّمَآءِ وَٱلطَّارِقِ
- 86:2 وَمَآ أَدْرَىٰكَ مَا ٱلطَّارِقُ
- 86:3 ٱلنَّجْمُ ٱلثَّاقِبُ
- 86:4 إِن كُلُّ نَفْسٍۢ لَّمَّا عَلَيْهَا حَافِظٌۭ
- 86:5 فَلْيَنظُرِ ٱلْإِنسَٰنُ مِمَّ خُلِقَ
- 86:6 خُلِقَ مِن مَّآءٍۢ دَافِقٍۢ
- 86:7 يَخْرُجُ مِنۢ بَيْنِ ٱلصُّلْبِ وَٱلتَّرَآئِبِ
- 86:8 إِنَّهُۥ عَلَىٰ رَجْعِهِۦ لَقَادِرٌۭ
- 86:9 يَوْمَ تُبْلَى ٱلسَّرَآئِرُ
- 86:10 فَمَا لَهُۥ مِن قُوَّةٍۢ وَلَا نَاصِرٍۢ
- 86:11 وَٱلسَّمَآءِ ذَاتِ ٱلرَّجْعِ
- 86:12 وَٱلْأَرْضِ ذَاتِ ٱلصَّدْعِ
- 86:13 إِنَّهُۥ لَقَوْلٌۭ فَصْلٌۭ
- 86:14 وَمَا هُوَ بِٱلْهَزْلِ
- 86:15 إِنَّهُمْ يَكِيدُونَ كَيْدًۭا
- 86:16 وَأَكِيدُ كَيْدًۭا
- 86:17 فَمَهِّلِ ٱلْكَٰفِرِينَ أَمْهِلْهُمْ رُوَيْدًۢا


===== _commentary/v16/work/s086/surah.r2/dictionary.md =====
# Dictionary: every branch of every focus root

Every root of the surah's words, each once, headed by the ayah and word where it first occurs; it also serves the later words listed with it.
One entry per branch: a Turkish label and Turkish glosses of its attested senses, then the classical dictionaries' own phrases with their source tags (ayn, sihah, tahdhib, maqayis, mufradat, jamhara). [kalıp] marks a sense the dictionaries attest only inside a fixed expression.
Identity roots come from the quran-data gateway. Word-scoped alternatives are cited analyses of this
exact word. **Echo roots** are observed but withheld mappings: sound-family candidates, not identity.

  إطافة شيء بشيء والإكليل منزل من منازل القمر والسحاب يدور بالمكان (maqayis)؛ الإكليل شبه عصابة مزينة بالجواهر والإكليل من منازل القمر وروضة مكللة حفت بالنور (ayn)؛ الإكليل شبه عصابة ويسمى التاج إكليلا والإكليل منزل والسحاب كأن غشاء ألبسه وروضة مكللة وسحاب مكلل (sihah)؛ الغمام المكلل السحابة تكون حولها قطع والإكليل شبه عصابة والإكليل منزل (tahdhib)؛ الإكليل سمي بذلك لإطافته بالرأس (mufradat)

  كلل حمل ولعله أن يكون من المتضادات (maqayis)؛ المكلل الجاد حمل فكلل مضى قدما وقد يكون كلل بمعنى جبن (sihah)؛ المكلل الذي يحمل فلا يرجع حتى يقع بقرنه وكلل فلان فلانا لم يطعه (tahdhib)

(source: latent_activation/network/v3/reviews/s086/reader_a_pilot.md)

# S086 Semantic Channel Discovery

## Parent Channels

### 1. Overhead Light and Celestial Disclosure
- Semantic invariant: What is above becomes legible by piercing, emerging, or flashing through a cover.
- Surface relation: direct; sky and night visitor at 86:1-2, star and piercing light at 86:3.
- Surprising reach: The same disclosure pattern extends from a star through cloud clearing to the paired flash of lightning and a smile.

#### Subchannel A. The Night-Piercing Star
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A nocturnal body arrives overhead and pierces the surrounding darkness with concentrated light.
- Active motifs: night star `ط ر ق:B001/m02`; star `ن ج م:B001/m01`; piercing stellar light `ث ق ب:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ط ر ق` at 86:1-2; `ن ج م` and `ث ق ب` at 86:3
- Synthesis: The visitor image gives the star an arrival role, while the star and piercing-light motifs supply the visible body and its action on darkness.

#### Subchannel B. Earth Beneath an Enclosing Sky
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A lower earth is oriented beneath an elevated roof-like expanse that surrounds what lies below.
- Active motifs: lower earth opposite the sky `ء ر ض:B001/m01`; sky or roof `س م و:B004/m01`; elevated visible body `س م و:B002/m01`; encircling canopy `ك ل ل:B005/m02`
- Ayah anchors: `ء ر ض` at 86:12; `س م و` at 86:1 and 86:11; `ك ل ل` at 86:4
- Synthesis: Vertical opposition, elevation, and encirclement form one spatial model in which the sky is both a high visible region and a covering boundary.

#### Subchannel C. Clearing Cloud and Sudden Flash
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A covered sky opens, a bright mark appears, and the disclosure is registered as a sudden flash.
- Active motifs: cloud emergence and sky clearing `خ ر ج:B005/m01`; clearing after rain `ن ج م:B007/m01`; lightning flash `ك ل ل:B011/m02`; teeth revealed in a smile `ك ل ل:B011/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `خ ر ج` at 86:7; `ن ج م` at 86:3; `ك ل ل` at 86:4
- Synthesis: Clearing and flashing share the operation of a bright form becoming visible through a previously closed or dark field; the smile preserves the same reveal at bodily scale.

#### Subchannel D. Crescent, Daylight, and Breathing Dawn
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Hidden nocturnal light yields to a dawn that opens, spreads, and becomes day.
- Active motifs: concealed crescent `س ر ر:B004/m01`; daylight `ي و م:B001/m01`; daybreak splitting open `ص د ع:B006/m01`; dawn breathing outward `ن ف س:B009/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `س ر ر` and `ي و م` at 86:9; `ص د ع` at 86:12; `ن ف س` at 86:4
- Synthesis: The crescent marks restrained light, while splitting and breathing describe dawn as an aperture whose illumination expands into daytime.

### 2. Time, Event, and Controlled Delay
- Semantic invariant: Time is organized as span, repeated instance, consequential event, and deliberately managed waiting.
- Surface relation: direct; day at 86:9 and commanded delay at 86:17.
- Surprising reach: Temporal ordering reaches from grammatical simultaneity to measured respite and advancement.

#### Subchannel A. Span, Instance, and Habit
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A duration is partitioned into occurrences whose repetition can become a settled routine.
- Active motifs: temporal span `ي و م:B002/m01`; single occurrence `ط ر ق:B010/m01`; recurrent habit `ط ر ق:B010/m02`; simultaneous “while” relation `ب ي ن:B010/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ي و م` at 86:9; `ط ر ق` at 86:1-2; `ب ي ن` at 86:7
- Synthesis: Duration supplies the field, the instance supplies its unit, habit supplies recurrence, and simultaneity locates one event within another.

#### Subchannel B. The Day as Ordeal
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A marked day becomes an event that exerts pressure and exposes the quality of action.
- Active motifs: major event or ordeal `ي و م:B003/m01`; testing that reveals condition `ب ل و:B002/m01`; strenuous handling `ك ي د:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ي و م` and surface `ب ل و` at 86:9; `ك ي د` at 86:15-16
- Synthesis: The event is not neutral duration: exertion and testing make it a consequential interval in which hidden quality becomes manifest.

#### Subchannel C. Respite, Gentleness, and Precedence
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Action is postponed without abandonment, using a measured interval to regulate what comes first and what follows.
- Active motifs: gentle delay `م ه ل:B001/m01`; gradual advancement or precedence `م ه ل:B002/m01`; deliberate gentleness `ر و د:B005/m01`; waiting `ن ظ ر:B002/m01`; granted respite `ن ف س:B015/m02`
- Ayah anchors: `م ه ل` and `ر و د` at 86:17; `ن ظ ر` at 86:5; `ن ف س` at 86:4
- Synthesis: Delay, waiting, and precedence form a single pacing mechanism: time is opened, ordered, and used as an instrument of controlled response.

### 3. Earth, Germination, and Ripening
- Semantic invariant: The ground covers latent life, opens under growth, and carries produce toward maturity.
- Surface relation: direct; earth and splitting at 86:12.
- Surprising reach: The earth scene includes stemless plants, truffles under crust, protective calyces, and water-filled ripening fruit.

#### Subchannel A. Fertile Ground and Low Growth
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Soft ground supports vegetation that rises without a woody stem.
- Active motifs: fertile yielding earth `ء ر ض:B002/m01`; soil-borne plant `ت ر ب:B007/m01`; stemless growth `ن ج م:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ء ر ض` at 86:12; `ت ر ب` at 86:7; `ن ج م` at 86:3
- Synthesis: Ground quality, plant setting, and the emerging low plant form a compact cultivation scene.

#### Subchannel B. Covered Seed and Splitting Shoot
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A cultivator covers seed, after which the plant breaks the earth's surface and emerges.
- Active motifs: farmer covering seed `ك ف ر:B008/m01`; plant splitting earth `ص د ع:B003/m01`; emergence into view `ن ج م:B002/m01`; rain-fed vegetation `س م و:B004/m03`
- Ayah anchors: `ك ف ر` at 86:17; `ص د ع` and `ء ر ض` at 86:12; `ن ج م` at 86:3; `س م و` at 86:1 and 86:11
- Synthesis: Burial, moisture, rupture, and emergence create a causal sequence from concealed seed to visible growth.

#### Subchannel C. Truffle, Calyx, and Fruit Filling
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Produce forms under or within a protective skin and becomes full as moisture and ripeness increase.
- Active motifs: truffle beneath an earthen crust `س ر ر:B013/m01`; soil matrix `ت ر ب:B001/m01`; enclosing calyx `ك ف ر:B010/m01`; fruit filling with water `م و ه:B002/m02`; ripened luster `م و ه:B006/m02`
- Ayah anchors: `س ر ر` at 86:9; `ت ر ب` at 86:7; `ك ف ر` at 86:17; `م و ه` at 86:6
- Synthesis: Crust and calyx protect what is forming, while internal water and visible luster mark the transition to maturity.

#### Subchannel D. Holding to the Ground
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A body settles onto land and remains attached rather than continuing in motion.
- Active motifs: clinging or lingering on earth `ء ر ض:B006/m01`; soil as contact surface `ت ر ب:B001/m01`; arrival onto land `ن ص ر:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ء ر ض` at 86:12; `ت ر ب` at 86:7; `ن ص ر` at 86:10

### 4. Settlement, Territory, and Livelihood
- Semantic invariant: Land becomes social space through arrival, habitation, territorial division, and material fortune.
- Surface relation: indirect; earth at 86:12, the human at 86:5, and aid or arrival imagery through `ن ص ر` at 86:10.
- Surprising reach: Settlement imagery extends to strangers, remote villages, named regions, poverty, wealth, and returning migration.

- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A newcomer reaches inhabited land and is distinguished from those already familiar with it.
- Active motifs: nocturnal visitor `ط ر ق:B001/m01`; stranger or alien `ء ر ض:B004/m01`; human as opposed to wild beings `ء ن س:B001/m01`; arrival on land `ن ص ر:B003/m01`

#### Subchannel B. Tract, Village, and Remote Region

- Ayah anchors: `ب ي ن` at 86:7; `ص د ع` and `ء ر ض` at 86:12; `ك ف ر` at 86:17

#### Subchannel C. Poverty, Barren Land, and Wealth
- Reading type: latent/lexical

#### Subchannel D. Named Places and Returning Migration
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A group leaves a named region and later returns along a remembered migratory course.

### 5. Water Circulation and Sustenance
- Semantic invariant: Water appears, returns, moves through channels, and sustains bodies through drinking and irrigation.
- Surface relation: direct; gushing water at 86:6 and returning sky at 86:11.
- Surprising reach: The same circulation includes wells, tunnels, tributaries, buckets, measured gulps, turbid pools, and spreading waves.

#### Subchannel A. Returning Rain and Refilled Water
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Water descends from the sky, reappears in the ground, and renews a previously depleted supply.
- Active motifs: recurring rainwater `ر ج ع:B006/m01`; rain as aid `ن ص ر:B004/m01`; rain-bearing sky `س م و:B004/m02`; water appearing and filling `م و ه:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ر ج ع` and `س م و` at 86:11; `ن ص ر` at 86:10; `م و ه` at 86:6
- Synthesis: Return, descent, and refilling describe rain as a recurrent transfer between sky, ground, and stored water.

#### Subchannel B. Tributary, Tunnel, and Basin
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Water follows a feeder channel through an opening into a basin or well.
- Active motifs: tributary `ن ص ر:B007/m01`; mountain tunnel `ث ق ب:B006/m01`; rock basin or well `خ ل ق:B011/m01`; trampled or turbid watercourse `ط ر ق:B008/m01`

#### Subchannel C. Irrigation, Bucket, and Drink

#### Subchannel D. Gush, Burst, and Spreading Wave
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Contained liquid is released suddenly, then expands outward as a flow or wave.

- Ayah anchors: `د ف ق` at 86:6; `ط ر ق` at 86:1-2; `ن ف س` at 86:4

### 6. Measure, Form, and Capacity
- Semantic invariant: Things become usable or intelligible through estimation, proportion, bounded extent, and fitted form.
- Surface relation: direct; creation at 86:5-6 and capacity at 86:8.
- Surprising reach: Measurement reaches from cutting cloth and spacing beads to personal aptitude, compact physique, and imperforate crystal.

#### Subchannel A. Estimation Before Cutting
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A maker calculates dimensions before cutting or producing an object.

- Ayah anchors: `خ ل ق` at 86:5-6; `ق د ر` at 86:8
- Synthesis: Quantity sets the bound, calculation selects the dimensions, and estimation precedes the irreversible act of cutting.

### 7. Rupture, Wear, and Repair
- Semantic invariant: Material integrity changes through cracking, piercing, dislocation, abrasion, and restorative joining.
- Surface relation: direct; splitting at 86:12 and separation at 86:13.
- Surprising reach: The rupture pattern extends from rock and cloth to joints, blunt blades, and recycled material.

#### Subchannel A. Crack and Separation

- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Expended organic material returns to the ground and can be worked again.
- Active motifs: returned or recycled matter `ر ج ع:B015/m01`; dung or worked waste `ر ج ع:B015/m02`; soil receiving matter `ت ر ب:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ر ج ع` at 86:8 and 86:11; `ت ر ب` at 86:7
- Synthesis: Return is material rather than spatial here: waste re-enters the earth as matter available for another cycle.

### 11. Implements, Fire, and Directed Force
- Semantic invariant: Hand-held or mechanical implements concentrate motion into turning, striking, piercing, binding, or ignition.
- Surface relation: indirect; the striking visitor root at 86:1-2, piercing at 86:3, and exertion at 86:15-16.
- Surprising reach: The implement scene joins crank and comb, club and hammer, rope and net, and a delayed fire drill.

#### Subchannel A. Crank, Axle, and Comb
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A pointed or toothed implement is worked through controlled rotation around a handle or axle.
- Active motifs: crank or rotating implement `ر و د:B006/m01`; horn or pointed comb-tool `د ر ي:B004/m01`; working face or side `ء ن س:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ر و د` at 86:17; `د ر ي` at 86:2; `ء ن س` at 86:5
- Synthesis: Handle, working edge, orientation, and rotation define a coherent manual mechanism.

#### Subchannel B. Hammer and Wooden Club
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A rigid wooden implement transfers a hand stroke into impact.
- Active motifs: hammering strike `ط ر ق:B003/m01`; wooden club or bat `ق و ل:B008/m01`; hardness `ص ل ب:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ط ر ق` at 86:1-2; `ق و ل` at 86:13; `ص ل ب` at 86:7
- Synthesis: Hard material, a grasped implement, and directed impact produce a single striking-tool scene.

#### Subchannel C. Fire Drilling and Delayed Ignition
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A hollow firestick is worked until friction finally produces penetrating flame.
- Active motifs: hollow firestick `س ر ر:B008/m01`; reluctant or delayed firestick `ك ي د:B006/m01`; ignition and piercing flame `ث ق ب:B002/m02`
- Ayah anchors: `س ر ر` at 86:9; `ك ي د` at 86:15-16; `ث ق ب` at 86:3
- Synthesis: The hollow tool receives repeated effort, delay marks failed early friction, and ignition is the eventual outcome.

#### Subchannel D. Rope Strands and Net Assembly
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Separate fibers are strengthened by combination and arranged into a catching or sheltering mesh.
- Active motifs: rope strands and tensile strength `ق و ي:B001/m02`; net or tent-screen `ك ل ل:B006/m01`; snare `ط ر ق:B013/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ق و ي` at 86:10; `ك ل ل` at 86:4; `ط ر ق` at 86:1-2
- Synthesis: Twisted strands provide strength, mesh supplies structure, and tension gives the assembled material its trapping function.

### 12. Movement, Tracks, and Return
- Semantic invariant: Motion is understood through departure, oscillation, trace-making, gait, turning, and eventual return.
- Surface relation: direct; return at 86:8 and 86:11 and the traveling visitor at 86:1-2.
- Surprising reach: Return extends to migration, bodily gait, retraced courses, scattered groups, and rebellion from an origin.

#### Subchannel A. Return, Turning, and Back-and-Forth Motion
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A mover leaves a position, turns, and comes back along an opposed course.
- Active motifs: return or restoration `ر ج ع:B001/m01`; turning aside `ص د ع:B010/m01`; back-and-forth motion `ر و د:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ر ج ع` at 86:8 and 86:11; `ص د ع` at 86:12; `ر و د` at 86:17
- Synthesis: Turning reverses direction, oscillation repeats the reversal, and return completes the course at its prior point.

#### Subchannel B. Tracks, Procession, and Scattering
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A moving group leaves traces, forms a procession, then disperses in different directions.
- Active motifs: tracks `ط ر ق:B009/m01`; procession `ط ر ق:B009/m02`; group scattering `ص د ع:B005/m01`; people dispersed in all directions `ب ل و:B009/m01`; scattering variant `ب ل ي:B008/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ط ر ق` at 86:1-2; `ص د ع` at 86:12; surface `ب ل و` at 86:9; `ب ل ي` is its lexical variant
- Synthesis: Ordered passage and dispersal are opposite configurations of collective motion, both legible through the traces they leave.

#### Subchannel C. Rapid Travel, Gait, and Droop
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A traveling body is read through speed, repeated footfall, and the weakening or lowering of posture.
- Active motifs: rapid travel `د ف ق:B003/m01`; characteristic gait `ر ج ع:B008/m01`; limp or drooping movement `ط ر ق:B006/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `د ف ق` at 86:6; `ر ج ع` at 86:8 and 86:11; `ط ر ق` at 86:1-2
- Synthesis: Speed and gait describe active locomotion, while droop marks its compromised or exhausted form.

#### Subchannel D. Departure and Rebellion from Origin
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A participant exits an established position and separates from its governing source.
- Active motifs: departure or rebellion from origin `خ ر ج:B006/m01`; setting out `ف ص ل:B010/m01`; physical exit `خ ر ج:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `خ ر ج` at 86:7; `ف ص ل` at 86:13
- Synthesis: Exit becomes rebellion when spatial departure also breaks an originating relation or authority.

#### Subchannel E. Returning Migration
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Migratory creatures leave a region and later reappear on a recurrent route.
- Active motifs: returning birds `ر ج ع:B012/m01`; named destination regions `ت ر ب:B008/m01`; route traces `ط ر ق:B009/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ر ج ع` at 86:8 and 86:11; `ت ر ب` at 86:7; `ط ر ق` at 86:1-2
- Synthesis: Region, route, departure, and cyclical reappearance form a complete migratory pattern.

### 13. Hunting, Targeting, and Pasture
- Semantic invariant: Acquisition requires locating a target, approaching under cover, and controlling a route of capture or grazing.
- Surface relation: indirect; knowing at 86:2, the night visitor at 86:1-2, and elevated visibility at 86:1 and 86:11.
- Surprising reach: The acquisition scene includes stalking, traps, noble quarry, ibex, and scouting for pasture.

#### Subchannel A. Stalking, Trap, and Hunter
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A hunter approaches under cover and channels prey into a prepared snare.
- Active motifs: stalking prey covertly `د ر ي:B003/m01`; trap or snare `ط ر ق:B013/m01`; hunters `س م و:B006/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `د ر ي` at 86:2; `ط ر ق` at 86:1-2; `س م و` at 86:1 and 86:11
- Synthesis: Concealed approach, prepared capture point, and hunting agent supply the essential roles of one hunting scene.

#### Subchannel B. Target, Raid, and Ibex Quarry
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A selected target is pursued as valued or difficult quarry.
- Active motifs: target or raiding objective `د ر ي:B002/m01`; noble target `ص د ع:B011/m01`; ibex `ص د ع:B008/m02`; elite or choicest part `س ر ر:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `د ر ي` at 86:2; `ص د ع` at 86:12; `س ر ر` at 86:9
- Synthesis: Selection, value, and pursuit convert a general hunt into an attack on a distinguished quarry.

#### Subchannel C. Pasture Scouting
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A scout searches ahead for grazing land before directing animals toward it.
- Active motifs: seeking and scouting pasture `ر و د:B003/m01`; fertile land `ء ر ض:B002/m01`; docile grazing camel `ت ر ب:B009/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ر و د` at 86:17; `ء ر ض` at 86:12; `ت ر ب` at 86:7
- Synthesis: Search, viable ground, and manageable livestock form a husbandry scene centered on locating sustenance.

### 14. Vision, Watching, and Discernment
- Semantic invariant: Seeing ranges from sensory registration through focused inspection to surveillance, reflection, and harmful gaze.
- Surface relation: direct; the command to look and the human observer at 86:5.
- Surprising reach: The visual channel reaches into pupil imagery, mirrors, evil-eye discoloration, expert penetration, bodily facing, and fatal glance.

#### Subchannel A. Sensing and Deliberate Sight
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A human detects something by sight or hearing and subjects it to attentive inspection.
- Active motifs: sensory detection and scrutiny `ء ن س:B002/m01`; deliberate seeing and reflection `ن ظ ر:B001/m01`; knowledge gained `د ر ي:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ء ن س` and `ن ظ ر` at 86:5; `د ر ي` at 86:2
- Synthesis: Detection initiates the act, looking sustains attention, and knowledge is its cognitive outcome.

#### Subchannel B. Pupil, Reflected Figure, and Mirror
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A viewer's image appears in the dark pupil or a water-clear reflective surface.
- Active motifs: pupil as reflected human image `ء ن س:B005/m01`; pupil `ن ظ ر:B008/m01`; mirror `م و ه:B007/m02`
- Ayah anchors: `ء ن س` and `ن ظ ر` at 86:5; `م و ه` at 86:6
- Synthesis: Eye and mirror share a small clear or dark surface that receives and returns the viewer's figure.

#### Subchannel C. Evil Eye, Pallor, and Ocular Defect
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A harmful gaze leaves a visible defect or pallor in the one observed.
- Active motifs: evil-eye strike `ن ف س:B003/m01`; pallor or defect from a glance `ن ظ ر:B007/m01`; diseased or defective eye `ر و د:B007/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ن ف س` at 86:4; `ن ظ ر` and `ء ن س` at 86:5; `ر و د` at 86:17
- Synthesis: Gaze acts as cause, ocular or bodily discoloration as sign, and defect as the enduring state.

#### Subchannel D. Watcher, Persistence, and Expert Insight
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A vigilant observer maintains attention until hidden detail is penetrated and understood.
- Active motifs: watcher or sentinel `ن ظ ر:B009/m01`; persistent retention `ح ف ظ:B003/m01`; penetrating insight `ث ق ب:B003/m02`; expert who reaches inward `س ر ر:B014/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ن ظ ر` at 86:5; `ح ف ظ` at 86:4; `ث ق ب` at 86:3; `س ر ر` at 86:9
- Synthesis: Sustained watching supplies duration, while penetration and expertise describe the depth of the resulting discernment.

#### Subchannel E. Facing and Opposed Orientation
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Bodies or surfaces are arranged toward, away from, or opposite one another.
- Active motifs: facing or opposition `ن ظ ر:B003/m01`; human-facing side `ء ن س:B004/m01`; lower earth opposed to sky `ء ر ض:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ن ظ ر` and `ء ن س` at 86:5; `ء ر ض` at 86:12
- Synthesis: Orientation is relational: a visible face or side is defined by what it confronts across an axis.

#### Subchannel F. Fatal Glance
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A single directed look acts upon a selected target with lethal force.
- Active motifs: fatal glance `ن ظ ر:B005/m01`; selected target `د ر ي:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ن ظ ر` at 86:5; `د ر ي` at 86:2
- Synthesis: Target selection and the causally potent glance form a compact scene in which seeing becomes an act rather than passive perception.

### 15. Thought, Will, and Deliberation
- Semantic invariant: Inner content is retained, formed into intention, tested through inquiry, and converted into decision.
- Surface relation: indirect; knowing at 86:2, looking at 86:5, capacity at 86:8, and gradual action at 86:17.
- Surprising reach: The inner channel includes silent speech, appropriated statements, negotiation, logical supposition, and dull-minded verbosity.

#### Subchannel A. Memory and Inner Thought
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Knowledge is retained internally before any outward expression.
- Active motifs: memorized retention `ح ف ظ:B002/m01`; thought and discrimination within the self `ن ف س:B013/m01`; unspoken inner statement `ق و ل:B012/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ح ف ظ` and `ن ف س` at 86:4; `ق و ل` at 86:13
- Synthesis: Retention preserves content, the inner self evaluates it, and silent speech gives it propositional form.

#### Subchannel B. Will, Essence, and Appropriated Speech
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A person draws an idea inward, makes it part of the self, and forms an intention around it.
- Active motifs: will or intention `ر و د:B001/m01`; essence or very self `ن ف س:B012/m01`; drawing a statement into oneself `ق و ل:B006/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ر و د` at 86:17; `ن ف س` at 86:4; `ق و ل` at 86:13
- Synthesis: Inward appropriation links received speech to identity and then to volitional commitment.

#### Subchannel C. Planning, Debate, and Negotiation
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Alternatives are calculated, argued, and negotiated before a course is selected.
- Active motifs: calculation and planning `ق د ر:B005/m01`; debate `ن ظ ر:B010/m01`; negotiation `ق و ل:B009/m01`; persuasion `ر و د:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ق د ر` at 86:8; `ن ظ ر` at 86:5; `ق و ل` at 86:13; `ر و د` at 86:17
- Synthesis: Calculation frames the options, debate tests them publicly, negotiation adjusts positions, and persuasion seeks the decision.

#### Subchannel D. Inquiry and Supposition
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An interrogative opens an unknown, and a provisional judgment answers without full certainty.
- Active motifs: interrogative “what?” `ذ و و:B004/m01`; supposition expressed as saying `ق و ل:B011/m01`; temporal or clause frame `ب ي ن:B010/m01`
- Ayah anchors: exact `ذَاتِ` occurs at 86:11-12 while the interrogative branch is lexical; `ق و ل` at 86:13; `ب ي ن` at 86:7
- Synthesis: The question establishes an information gap, while supposition offers a revisable completion inside a defined clause or moment.

#### Subchannel E. Dull Mind and Excess Speech
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Verbal abundance is paired with weak discernment.
- Active motifs: dull or water-heavy mind `م و ه:B008/m01`; loquacious speaker `ق و ل:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `م و ه` at 86:6; `ق و ل` at 86:13
- Synthesis: The portrait contrasts fluent output with limited acuity, forming a coherent character scene rather than a general speech category.

### 16. Speech, Explanation, and Reply
- Semantic invariant: Speech is produced, clarified, judged, answered, and distorted within an exchange.
- Surface relation: direct; decisive utterance at 86:13 and its contrast with jest at 86:14.
- Surprising reach: The discourse sequence includes tongue as instrument, explicit signification, proclamation, affirmative reversal, fabricated attribution, and circulating rumor.

#### Subchannel A. Tongue and Articulated Utterance
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: The tongue shapes internal content into audible words.
- Active motifs: voiced utterance `ق و ل:B001/m01`; tongue as speech instrument `ق و ل:B002/m01`; explicit expression `ب ي ن:B005/m01`; tongue or articulator `ف ص ل:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ق و ل` and `ف ص ل` at 86:13; `ب ي ن` at 86:7
- Synthesis: Instrument, articulation, and emitted statement form the production side of speech.

#### Subchannel B. Explanation and Signification
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An obscure matter is unfolded until its details and intended indication become clear.
- Active motifs: disclosure `ب ي ن:B004/m01`; explanation `ب ي ن:B005/m01`; detailed analysis `ف ص ل:B013/m01`; signification `ق و ل:B014/m01`; bringing hidden content out `خ ر ج:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ب ي ن` and `خ ر ج` at 86:7; `ف ص ل` and `ق و ل` at 86:13
- Synthesis: Extraction exposes the matter, analysis separates its parts, and signification links the clarified statement to what it means.

#### Subchannel C. Proclamation and Judgment
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A conclusion is announced openly and made binding through decisive separation.
- Active motifs: public proclamation `ص د ع:B004/m01`; decisive judgment `ف ص ل:B002/m01`; exercising judgment or domination `ق و ل:B010/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ص د ع` at 86:12; `ف ص ل` and `ق و ل` at 86:13
- Synthesis: Proclamation gives the judgment public form, while decisive separation determines the outcome.

#### Subchannel D. Reply and Affirmative Reversal
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A prior negative or question receives a reply that reverses denial and establishes affirmation.
- Active motifs: reply or answer `ر ج ع:B005/m01`; affirmative answer after negation `ب ل و:B006/m01`; affirmative lexical variant `ب ل ي:B010/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ر ج ع` at 86:8 and 86:11; surface `ب ل و` at 86:9; `ب ل ي` is its lexical variant
- Synthesis: Reply returns speech to its source, while the affirmative particle performs a precise logical reversal.

#### Subchannel E. Fabrication, False Attribution, and Rumor
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A statement is invented, assigned to someone who did not say it, and circulated socially.
- Active motifs: fabrication of lies `خ ل ق:B007/m01`; false attribution `ق و ل:B005/m01`; circulating report `ق و ل:B007/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `خ ل ق` at 86:5-6; `ق و ل` at 86:13
- Synthesis: Invention creates false content, attribution supplies a false source, and rumor distributes it among hearers.

### 17. Grammar, Naming, and Patterned Sound
- Semantic invariant: Language is formally organized through connectors, separators, naming, measured text, and recurrent sound.
- Surface relation: direct; interrogative framing at 86:2 and separated utterance at 86:13.
- Surprising reach: Formal organization extends from deixis and logical definition to verse boundaries, rhyme defects, coda letters, echoes, notes, and cries.

#### Subchannel A. Deixis, Interrogative, and Grammatical Separation
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A clause points to a referent, asks about it, and separates its grammatical units.
- Active motifs: demonstrative pointer `ذ و و:B003/m01`; interrogative form `ذ و و:B004/m01`; temporal clause relation `ب ي ن:B010/m01`; grammatical separator `ف ص ل:B015/m01`
- Ayah anchors: exact `ذَاتِ` at 86:11-12; `ب ي ن` at 86:7; `ف ص ل` at 86:13
- Synthesis: Deixis identifies, interrogation opens a slot for information, and separation marks the clause's internal structure.

#### Subchannel B. Relative Connector and Whole
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A connector attaches a dependent expression to a larger complete construction.
- Active motifs: relative connector `ذ و و:B002/m01`; whole formed by encompassing parts `ك ل ل:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: exact `ذَاتِ` at 86:11-12; `ك ل ل` at 86:4
- Synthesis: The connector attaches a dependent expression, while totality names the completed construction into which it is incorporated.

#### Subchannel C. Verse Boundary, Meter, and Coda
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A text is segmented into measured units whose ending sounds maintain or disrupt a pattern.
- Active motifs: verse separators `ف ص ل:B011/m01`; metrical or rhyme defect `ق و ي:B002/m01`; coda alif `خ ر ج:B011/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ف ص ل` and `ق و ل` at 86:13; `ق و ي` at 86:10; `خ ر ج` at 86:7
- Synthesis: Segmentation creates the textual unit, meter governs its internal timing, and the coda closes its audible form.

#### Subchannel D. Echo, Note, and Strained Cry
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A sounded impulse is projected, repeated, and returned through reverberation.
- Active motifs: echo or refrain `ر ج ع:B007/m01`; single musical note `ط ر ق:B014/m01`; strained cry `ك ي د:B005/m01`; voiced utterance `ق و ل:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ر ج ع` at 86:8 and 86:11; `ط ر ق` at 86:1-2; `ك ي د` at 86:15-16; `ق و ل` at 86:13
- Synthesis: Voice supplies the source, strain its force, the note its discrete pitch, and echo its returning acoustic trace.

#### Subchannel E. Name, Essence, and Definition
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A thing is identified by a name and delimited by a statement of what it is.
- Active motifs: name or appellation `س م و:B005/m01`; essential identity `ن ف س:B012/m01`; logical definition `ق و ل:B016/m01`; signification `ق و ل:B014/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `س م و` at 86:1 and 86:11; `ن ف س` at 86:4; `ق و ل` at 86:13
- Synthesis: Naming selects the referent, essence supplies the object of definition, and signification connects the defining words to it.

### 18. Testing, Truth, and Seriousness
- Semantic invariant: Hidden quality is exposed, stated decisively, and distinguished from evasion, jest, or concealment.
- Surface relation: direct; secrets tested at 86:9 and decisive, non-jesting speech at 86:13-14.
- Surprising reach: The disclosure frame includes examination, public excuse, oath, testimony, covering, extraction, and proclamation.

#### Subchannel A. Test That Reveals Secrets
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: Examination acts on concealed inward matter until its real quality appears.
- Active motifs: test revealing condition `ب ل و:B002/m01`; test variant `ب ل ي:B002/m01`; secret inward matter `س ر ر:B001/m01`; disclosure `ب ي ن:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: surface `ب ل و` and `س ر ر` at 86:9; `ب ل ي` is the lexical variant; `ب ي ن` at 86:7
- Synthesis: Secret is the concealed object, testing the operation, and disclosure the resulting change from hidden to manifest.

#### Subchannel B. Decisive Word, Not Jest
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A serious utterance separates truth from play and carries a binding conclusion.
- Active motifs: spoken statement `ق و ل:B001/m01`; decisive judgment `ف ص ل:B002/m01`; jest `ه ز ل:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ق و ل` and `ف ص ل` at 86:13; `ه ز ل` at 86:14
- Synthesis: The word is defined positively by decisiveness and negatively by the exclusion of levity.

#### Subchannel C. Excuse, Oath, and Removal of Blame
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A challenged person presents an explanation or oath so that blame can be tested and removed.
- Active motifs: declared excuse `ب ل و:B004/m01`; excuse variant `ب ل ي:B004/m01`; offered oath `ب ل و:B004/m02`; oath variant `ب ل ي:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: surface `ب ل و` at 86:9; `ب ل ي` is the lexical variant
- Synthesis: Explanation addresses the alleged fault, the oath raises the cost of falsehood, and acceptance restores standing.

#### Subchannel D. Covering and Public Revelation
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Concealed content is removed from its cover and announced openly.
- Active motifs: covering `ك ف ر:B001/m01`; extraction into view `خ ر ج:B002/m01`; proclamation `ص د ع:B004/m01`; disclosure `ب ي ن:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ك ف ر` at 86:17; `خ ر ج` and `ب ي ن` at 86:7; `ص د ع` at 86:12

### 19. Disposition, Merit, and Fidelity

- Ayah anchors: `خ ل ق` at 86:5-6; `ن ف س` at 86:4; `ر و د` at 86:17

#### Subchannel C. Fidelity, Chastity, and Loyal Guarding

#### Subchannel D. Indifference and Ingratitude
- Reading type: latent/lexical

- Active motifs: lack of concern `ب ل و:B007/m01`; indifference variant `ب ل ي:B009/m01`; ingratitude `ك ف ر:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: surface `ب ل و` at 86:9; `ب ل ي` is its lexical variant; `ك ف ر` at 86:17

#### Subchannel E. Familiarity, Joy, and Relief
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Recognition removes estrangement and opens an inward state of ease.
- Active motifs: familiarity dispelling loneliness `ء ن س:B003/m01`; joy and ease `س ر ر:B010/m01`; relief from distress `ن ف س:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ء ن س` at 86:5; `س ر ر` at 86:9; `ن ف س` at 86:4
- Synthesis: Familiarity changes the social setting, joy its affective result, and relief the release of prior constriction.

#### Subchannel F. Ardor, Anger, and Flush
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Strong inward heat appears outwardly as force and reddening.
- Active motifs: anger or ardor `ح ف ظ:B005/m01`; intense redness `ث ق ب:B004/m01`; courage and pride `ن ف س:B014/m02`

### 20. Rank, Authority, and Competition
- Semantic invariant: Social standing is produced through elevation, inherited distinction, command, possession, peer comparison, and public reputation.
- Surface relation: indirect; elevation through the sky at 86:1 and 86:11, strength and aid at 86:10, and speech at 86:13.
- Surprising reach: The status field includes nobility, elite cores, princes, dependents, cohorts, rivalry, rumor, and prized objects.

#### Subchannel A. Elevation, Fame, and Nobility
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A person rises into public visibility and is recognized as distinguished by rank or ancestry.
- Active motifs: social elevation `س م و:B001/m02`; fame and eminence `ث ق ب:B003/m01`; ancestry and nobility `ص ل ب:B007/m01`; prominence `ن ج م:B006/m02`
- Ayah anchors: `س م و` at 86:1 and 86:11; `ث ق ب` and `ن ج م` at 86:3; `ص ل ب` at 86:7
- Synthesis: Elevation supplies the spatial metaphor, fame the public visibility, and ancestry a durable basis for rank.

#### Subchannel B. Prince, Possessor, and Dependent
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A holder of effective speech commands resources and bears responsibility for dependents.
- Active motifs: prince or lesser king `ق و ل:B004/m01`; possessor `ذ و و:B001/m01`; dependent burden `ك ل ل:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ق و ل` at 86:13; exact `ذَاتِ` at 86:11-12; `ك ل ل` at 86:4
- Synthesis: Possession gives control, authoritative speech exercises it, and dependence defines the social obligations attached to rank.

#### Subchannel C. Elite Core
- Reading type: latent/lexical

- Active motifs: elite or choicest core `س ر ر:B005/m01`; nobility `ص ل ب:B007/m01`; whole containing parts `ك ل ل:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `س ر ر` at 86:9; `ص ل ب` at 86:7; `ك ل ل` at 86:4
- Synthesis: Core location, select value, and encompassing whole produce a center-periphery model of elite status.

#### Subchannel D. Peers, Equals, and Cohorts

- Active motifs: age peers `ت ر ب:B004/m01`; equal or counterpart `ن ظ ر:B006/m01`; organized groups `ك ل ل:B009/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ت ر ب` at 86:7; `ن ظ ر` at 86:5; `ك ل ل` at 86:4
- Synthesis: Equality supplies the relation, peer age a concrete basis, and grouping the social frame in which comparison occurs.

#### Subchannel E. Rivalry, Preciousness, and Reputation
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Competitors seek a valued object or standing whose desirability is amplified by public report.

- Ayah anchors: `س م و` at 86:1 and 86:11; `ن ف س` at 86:4; `ق و ل` at 86:13

### 21. Guardianship, Aid, and Strategy
- Semantic invariant: Protection combines attentive care, persistence, supporting force, delayed response, and counteraction.
- Surface relation: direct; guardian at 86:4, absent strength and helper at 86:10, scheming at 86:15-16, and delay at 86:17.
- Surprising reach: Protective action extends to stewardship, concern, vindication, upright stance, weapon readiness, and battle.

#### Subchannel A. Guarding, Stewardship, and Concern
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A custodian continuously tends something whose welfare is a genuine concern.
- Active motifs: guarding and tending `ح ف ظ:B001/m01`; sincere concern `ق و ل:B015/m01`; watcher `ن ظ ر:B009/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ح ف ظ` at 86:4; `ق و ل` at 86:13; `ن ظ ر` at 86:5
- Synthesis: Concern identifies the protected interest, watching detects threats, and tending supplies ongoing intervention.

#### Subchannel B. Persistence and Alertness

- Scene or process: Attention is maintained without lapse so that a changing situation remains under control.

- Ayah anchors: `ح ف ظ` at 86:4; `س ر ر` at 86:9
- Synthesis: Vigilance supplies readiness, persistence supplies duration, and expertise enables accurate response.

#### Subchannel C. Aid, Vindication, and Standing Upright
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Support reaches a pressured person, restores the right, and enables an upright stance.
- Active motifs: aid `ن ص ر:B001/m01`; vindication `ن ص ر:B002/m01`; standing upright `م ه ل:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ن ص ر` at 86:10; `م ه ل` at 86:17
- Synthesis: Aid supplies force, vindication repairs status, and upright posture embodies the restored outcome.

#### Subchannel D. Scheme and Deferred Countermove
- Reading type: surface-primary

- Active motifs: scheme and deferred punishment `ك ي د:B002/m01`; deliberate delay `م ه ل:B001/m01`; waiting `ن ظ ر:B002/m01`; gentle pacing `ر و د:B005/m01`

- Synthesis: Scheme establishes the contest, while waiting and delay convert time into strategic leverage.

#### Subchannel E. Weapon Readiness and Battle

- Active motifs: hand returning to a weapon `ر ج ع:B010/m01`; battle `ك ي د:B004/m01`; exercise of domination `ق و ل:B010/m01`; hard resistance `ص ل ب:B001/m01`

### 22. Kinship, Collective Order, and Separation
- Semantic invariant: Social groups are formed by lineage and proximity, maintained through connection, and altered by dispersal or legal separation.
- Surface relation: indirect; human identity at 86:5, between-ness at 86:7, and exposed inward relations at 86:9.
- Surprising reach: Collective imagery includes tribal names, collateral inheritance, confidants, geographic scattering, and revocable versus irrevocable divorce.

#### Subchannel A. Clan and Tribe
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Persons are organized into a named descent group with recognized subdivisions.
- Active motifs: clan `ط ر ق:B012/m01`; clan subdivision `ف ص ل:B009/m01`; named tribe `ب ل و:B008/m01`; tribal variant `ب ل ي:B007/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ط ر ق` at 86:1-2; `ف ص ل` at 86:13; surface `ب ل و` at 86:9; `ب ل ي` is its lexical variant
- Synthesis: Name, descent, and subdivision turn an aggregate of persons into a durable collective identity.

#### Subchannel B. Collateral Kin and Inheritance Connection

#### Subchannel D. Collective Scattering

#### Subchannel E. Divorce and Possible Return

### 23. Property, Exchange, and Restitution
- Semantic invariant: Goods and obligations are quantified, divided, negotiated, transferred, and returned.
- Surface relation: indirect; capacity and quantity at 86:8, strength at 86:10, and decisive settlement at 86:13.
- Surprising reach: Exchange imagery includes tax, shared-property auction, partner settlement, replacement, resale, and benefaction.

#### Subchannel A. Assessed Payment and Tax
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An obligation is quantified and collected as a known payment.
- Active motifs: tax or assessed payment `خ ر ج:B003/m01`; fixed quantity `ق د ر:B001/m02`

#### Subchannel B. Division, Auction, and Partner Settlement
- Reading type: latent/lexical

#### Subchannel D. Gift and Benefaction

#### Subchannel F. Palm-Shoot Transplant

- Synthesis: Agony supplies the terminal struggle, expulsion the event, and soul the departing participant.

### 29. Chance, Divination, and Play
- Semantic invariant: Uncertain outcomes are approached through casting, reading signs, wagering, or rule-bound manual play.
- Surface relation: indirect; star and piercing light at 86:3, knowing at 86:2, and forceful action at 86:15-16.
- Surprising reach: Procedures of uncertainty include pebbles, stars, spilled liquid, gambling arrows, and a bat-and-hand guessing game.

#### Subchannel A. Pebble Divination
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Pebbles are cast or struck and their resulting arrangement is interpreted.
- Active motifs: pebble divination `ط ر ق:B004/m01`; interpretive calculation `ق د ر:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ط ر ق` at 86:1-2; `ق د ر` at 86:8
- Synthesis: Casting produces a chance arrangement, and calculation converts that arrangement into a forecast.

#### Subchannel B. Astrology and Stargazing
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An observer watches celestial positions and calculates their supposed significance.
- Active motifs: astrology `ن ج م:B005/m01`; calculation `ق د ر:B005/m01`; watcher `ن ظ ر:B009/m01`; piercing star `ث ق ب:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ن ج م` and `ث ق ب` at 86:3; `ق د ر` at 86:8; `ن ظ ر` at 86:5
- Synthesis: Star supplies the sign, watching the observation, and calculation the interpretive procedure.

#### Subchannel C. Omen from Spilled Liquid
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A sudden spill is read as a favorable or unfavorable sign and may prompt a gift or response.
- Active motifs: omen from a spill `د ف ق:B007/m01`; sudden burst `د ف ق:B002/m01`; gift `ن ص ر:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `د ف ق` at 86:6; `ن ص ر` at 86:10
- Synthesis: The spill is the contingent event, omen its interpretation, and gift a possible social response to the perceived sign.

#### Subchannel D. Gambling Arrow
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A named arrow is drawn as a numbered share in a game of chance.
- Active motifs: fifth gambling arrow `ن ف س:B016/m01`; share division `خ ر ج:B012/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ن ف س` at 86:4; `خ ر ج` at 86:7
- Synthesis: The arrow supplies the randomized token, the draw the chance event, and division the allocation of outcome.

#### Subchannel E. Bat-and-Hand Game
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Players use a wooden bat or hand gesture in a rule-bound guessing and striking game.
- Active motifs: hand guessing game `خ ر ج:B010/m01`; wooden bat `ق و ل:B008/m01`; striking action `ط ر ق:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `خ ر ج` at 86:7; `ق و ل` at 86:13; `ط ر ق` at 86:1-2
- Synthesis: Implement, hand action, and guessed outcome form a concrete recreational procedure.

### 30. Belief, Rejection, and Atonement
- Semantic invariant: Religious or moral alignment is marked by acceptance, covering, repudiation, accusation, expiation, doctrine, and confessional sign.
- Surface relation: direct; disbelievers at 86:17 and decisive speech at 86:13-14.
- Surprising reach: The belief field includes ingratitude, repentance, coerced disobedience, oath-expiration, doctrinal positions, crucifixion, the cross, and Christian naming.

#### Subchannel A. Disbelief as Covering
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A known claim is covered over and refused acknowledgment.
- Active motifs: physical covering `ك ف ر:B001/m01`; disbelief `ك ف ر:B003/m01`; revealed claim `ب ي ن:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ك ف ر` at 86:17; `ب ي ن` at 86:7
- Synthesis: Covering supplies the operative metaphor, revelation the known object, and disbelief the resulting stance toward it.

#### Subchannel B. Ingratitude, Disavowal, and Repentant Return
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A person repudiates a prior bond or benefit, then may reverse direction through repentance.
- Active motifs: ingratitude `ك ف ر:B004/m01`; disavowal `ك ف ر:B005/m01`; repentance or return `ر ج ع:B003/m01`; turning aside and back `ص د ع:B010/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ك ف ر` at 86:17; `ر ج ع` at 86:8 and 86:11; `ص د ع` at 86:12
- Synthesis: Disavowal breaks acknowledgment, turning changes orientation, and repentance restores the abandoned relation.

#### Subchannel C. Accusation and Coerced Disobedience
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: One party labels another an unbeliever or drives that person into transgression.
- Active motifs: accusation of disbelief `ك ف ر:B006/m01`; forcing disobedience `ك ف ر:B007/m01`; false attribution `ق و ل:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ك ف ر` at 86:17; `ق و ل` at 86:13
- Synthesis: Attribution supplies the charge, accusation fixes identity, and coercion seeks conduct consistent with the imposed label.

#### Subchannel D. Expiation and Oath
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A binding oath or fault creates an obligation discharged through expiation.
- Active motifs: expiation `ك ف ر:B009/m01`; offered oath `ب ل ي:B005/m01`; oath and excuse `ب ل و:B004/m02`; vigilant fulfillment `ح ف ظ:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ك ف ر` at 86:17; surface `ب ل و` at 86:9; `ب ل ي` is its lexical variant; `ح ف ظ` at 86:4
- Synthesis: Oath creates the obligation, vigilance governs fulfillment, and expiation repairs breach.

#### Subchannel E. Doctrinal Position
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A person adopts and states a defined position within a tradition.
- Active motifs: doctrine or school position `ق و ل:B013/m01`; logical definition `ق و ل:B016/m01`; whole doctrinal system `ك ل ل:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ق و ل` at 86:13; `ك ل ل` at 86:4
- Synthesis: Definition gives the proposition boundaries, adoption makes it a position, and the whole system supplies its larger intellectual frame.

#### Subchannel F. Crucifixion, Cross, and Christian Identity
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A cross-shaped sign links the act of crucifixion to a named confessional identity.
- Active motifs: crucifixion `ص ل ب:B005/m01`; cross sign `ص ل ب:B006/m01`; Christianity or Christian naming `ن ص ر:B006/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ص ل ب` at 86:7; `ن ص ر` at 86:10
- Synthesis: The execution form supplies the object, the cross its portable sign, and Christian naming its communal identification.

## Standalone Subchannels

### S1. Grave-Tethered Camel
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A camel is tied at its owner's grave and left without food or water until death.
- Active motifs: grave-tethered camel `ب ل و:B005/m01`; lexical variant of the grave camel `ب ل ي:B006/m01`; grave `ك ف ر:B012/m02`
- Ayah anchors: surface `ب ل و` at 86:9; `ب ل ي` is its lexical variant; `ك ف ر` at 86:17
- Synthesis: Owner's grave, tethered animal, deprivation, and death form a complete funerary custom distinct from ordinary husbandry.

### S2. Two-Sided Carrying Bag
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A paired pouch is balanced across two sides for carrying.
- Active motifs: two-sided bag or paired pouch `خ ر ج:B009/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `خ ر ج` at 86:7
- Synthesis: The branch preserves a concrete container whose defining mechanism is bilateral balance around a carrier.

### S3. Bicolor Mottling
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A body or animal surface displays two contrasting colors in irregular patches.
- Active motifs: bicolor mottling or variegation `خ ر ج:B007/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `خ ر ج` at 86:7
- Synthesis: Color contrast and patch distribution form a coherent visible state distinct from rupture, disease, or applied decoration.

### S4. Wood-Gnawing Termite
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A termite consumes wood from within and progressively weakens it.
- Active motifs: termite that eats wood `ء ر ض:B010/m01`; wooden game-stick `ق و ل:B008/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ء ر ض` at 86:12; `ق و ل` at 86:13
- Synthesis: Insect, wooden object, hidden gnawing, and structural decay form a self-contained material-degradation scene.

### S5. Snake-Name Lexical Class
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A set of snake names is carried by the lexical branch independently of jest.
- Active motifs: snakes named by the branch `ه ز ل:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: `ه ز ل` at 86:14
- Synthesis: This is a coherent concrete creature class preserved as its own lexical channel rather than folded into the surface contrast with seriousness.
